a:T7c5,The core insight is to decompose the monolithic data mixture problem into two orthogonal sub-problems, supported by a closed-loop system with three modules: 1) Automated dataset-level assessment: Uses datasets as the minimal evaluation unit (aligns with industrial pipelines, avoids sample-level annotation noise) and adopts the LLM-as-Judge paradigm (Seed-1.6 model, 150 samples per dataset) to quantify three dimensions: capability classification (based on an extensible taxonomy defined via natural language, no manual labeling required, supports new capability categories), quality (4 dimensions, with hallucination penalty), and difficulty (6 dimensions, prioritizing cross-modal synthesis and prior knowledge requirements). Human alignment validation shows Spearman correlation r=0.82 for quality scores and r=0.75 for difficulty scores, meeting stability requirements for downstream optimization. 2) Decoupled mixture optimization: Inter-class ratios (token budget allocation across capability categories e.g., image-text vs video-text, grounding vs captioning) use coordinate-style single-variable iterative search, warm-started from the average of public reference recipes. Each round sweeps one category's ratio candidates while fixing others (with renormalization) and selects the value with best validation performance, avoiding multi-variable confounding. Intra-class ratios (e.g., mixing multiple OCR datasets) use constrained convex optimization, with an explicit entropy term in the objective to enforce diversity, weighted by quality and difficulty scores to avoid redundant stacking. 3) Standardized data admission: Fixes inter-class ratios, total token budget, and intra-class strategy, turning new dataset addition into a single-variable controlled intervention whose impact can be measured directly without full recipe re-sweeping. Multiple-Choice-Question (MCQ) validation is used to evaluate proxy checkpoints to reduce dependence on free-form instruction following.b:T47d,Key reported results include: 1) Automated dataset-level scores show strong correlation with human consensus: Spearman r=0.82 for quality, r=0.75 for difficulty, meeting stability requirements for downstream optimization. 2) Inter-class ratio optimization uses coordinate-style single-variable search, adjusting only one category's ratio per round to avoid multi-variable confounding, drastically reducing computational complexity compared to intractable high-dimensional joint search, with an interpretable and reproducible process. 3) Ratios searched on proxy-scale models transfer directly to larger model and data scales without additional tuning. 4) A VLM trained with 80B additional multimodal continued-pretraining tokens under the optimized recipe matches the performance of strong contemporary open-source baselines (LLaVA series, Qwen3-VL, etc.). 5) With fixed inter-class ratios, total token budget and intra-class strategy, new dataset addition becomes a single-variable controlled intervention, whose impact can be accurately measured without traditional expensive "restack and full retrain" ablations, greatly reducing evaluation cost.c:T502,Priority reading order: 1) Section 2.1 (Automated Dataset-Level Assessment) in Methodology: Focus on the natural-language-defined capability taxonomy design, LLM-as-Judge prompt templates (Appendix B), and human alignment validation setup — this is the foundational module that can be directly reused for other pretraining data pipelines. 2) Section 2.2 (Decoupled Mixture Optimization): Focus on the warm-start strategy and convergence criterion for inter-class single-variable search, the objective function design for intra-class convex optimization (especially the weight setting of the diversity entropy term), and constraint definitions — this is the core innovation, transferable to pure-text LLM data mixing. 3) Section 2.3 (Standardized Data Admission): Focus on the control variable design for single-variable intervention, the key to solving data iteration attribution. 4) Ablation studies in Experiments: Focus on cross-scale ratio transfer validation, and cost-performance tradeoff between decoupled and joint search. Points for further exploration: how validation set selection for inter-class search avoids overfitting, integration of difficulty scores with data curriculum learning, and the boundary of entropy term weight's suppression effect on redundant data.d:T74c,技术路线是一个端到端 CuraWeb curation framework。原始数据来自 2013–2024 年的 12 年 Common Crawl snapshots。先用 Resiliparse 从 WARC 抽取正文，去掉 header、footer 和广告噪声；再用 FastText 语言分类器保留 English confidence > 0.5 的文档；exact dedup 用全局 SHA-256 文档哈希；随后用扩展 URL blacklist 去掉 spam 和成人内容域。这个基础漏斗从 184.9B raw documents 压到 41.4B unique documents，保留率 22.4%；URL blacklist 后得到 39.1B documents，保留率 94.5%。

核心创新有三块。第一是 dual-track cleaning。规则过滤不再使用统一阈值，而是根据 domain 调整阈值，避免数学、代码等高价值领域因符号密度、长度分布或格式特征被误杀。模型驱动部分使用 data understanding model 给文档打多维标签，包括 writing quality、content value 和 domain tags。第二是 hybrid deduplication。传统 n-gram / LSH 能处理显性重复，但对两类 hidden redundancy 不够：一种是 templatized clusters，即页面结构高度相同但局部参数不同；另一种是 redundant crawling，即相似度刚好落在 LSH 阈值下方的重复抓取。CuraWeb 用 soft semantic deduplication 和 weighted voting：不是单条相似边超过阈值就删除，而是累计多个相似边的 penalty score，超过总阈值才判为冗余。这样比硬阈值更不容易误删边界样本。第三是 multi-objective sampling。writing quality 用 harmonic mean 聚合，因为任何一个严重缺陷都应显著拉低分数；content value 用线性求和，因为专业性、信息密度等维度可以累加贡献；再把质量指标与 diversity score 结合，保证 domain coverage。最终采用 Power Sampling，提高高信息量文档的抽样概率，同时不把分布完全压成单一高分区域。e:T7fa,The pipeline starts from twelve years of Common Crawl snapshots from 2013 to 2024. Main-body text is extracted from WARC files with Resiliparse, removing headers, footers, and advertisement noise. A FastText language classifier keeps English documents with confidence above 0.5. Global SHA-256 hashing performs exact document deduplication, reducing 184.9B raw documents to 41.4B unique documents, a 22.4% retention rate. An extended URL blacklist then removes spam and adult domains, leaving 39.1B documents with a 94.5% retention rate after that stage.

The main design has three components. First, dual-track cleaning combines domain-aware heuristic rules with model-driven document understanding. Instead of applying uniform thresholds, the rules are calibrated per domain so that math, code, and other structurally unusual but useful content are not removed simply because they violate generic web-text statistics. A data understanding model annotates documents with multi-aspect writing quality, content value, and domain tags. Second, hybrid deduplication combines n-gram-style filtering with semantic deduplication. The paper highlights two hard cases: templatized clusters, where pages share a structural skeleton but differ in local parameters, and redundant crawling cases that fall just below an LSH threshold. CuraWeb uses a soft semantic deduplication strategy based on weighted voting over multiple similarity edges. A document is removed only when its accumulated penalty exceeds a threshold, which reduces over-filtering compared with hard similarity cutoffs. Third, multi-objective sampling combines writing quality, content value, and diversity. Writing quality is aggregated with a harmonic mean to penalize severe defects on any single dimension. Content value is summed linearly to reward expertise and information density. A diversity score is added to preserve domain coverage, and Power Sampling increases the probability of selecting high-value documents without collapsing the sample into a narrow high-score slice.f:T714,JOLT follows the same high-level recipe as ToaST and ConvexTok—integer programming, LP relaxation, then rounding—but the formulation is specific to GL2R rather than split-tree or shortest-path inference. The model has two coupled sets of variables: token-selection variables indicating which candidate substrings enter the vocabulary, and segmentation-choice variables indicating which candidate segmentation is chosen for each pretoken. The objective minimizes frequency-weighted total token count.

The technical core is the greedy-consistency constraint. If a pretoken is assigned a particular segmentation, then at every segment start there must not exist a longer selected token that also matches the bytes at that position; otherwise GL2R would emit the longer token and the chosen segmentation would not be realizable. This is the mechanism that ties segmentation feasibility to vocabulary content.

A second important piece is scalability. A pretoken of length n has 2^(n-1) possible segmentations, so full enumeration is impossible. JOLT therefore works with a restricted candidate set and adds segmentations on demand, based on the optimization state, instead of instantiating the full combinatorial space. The corpus is first split into pretokens with a regex and aggregated by count; optimization is then run on the top-N most frequent pretokens. The LP solution is rounded to a fixed-size vocabulary, and the paper argues that rounding is not the main source of suboptimality because the rounded solutions stay extremely close to the LP bound. Relative to BPE, JOLT removes the dependence on local merge heuristics. Relative to UnigramLM, it is not a probabilistic latent-segmentation model. Relative to ConvexTok and ToaST, the feasibility constraints are driven by GL2R longest-match behavior.10:T4d3,The experiments focus on compression, LP-bound diagnostics, and rounding loss rather than downstream language-model quality. The authors evaluate four training scopes with N in {100k, 200k, 300k, 400k} high-frequency pretokens. At equal vocabulary size, JOLT reduces held-out greedy-L2R token count by up to 0.78% relative to BPE, and larger scopes are consistently as good or better.

The more informative result is the lower-bound analysis. On the training scope, BPE is already only about 1%–2% above the LP lower bound, indicating that BPE is near-optimal for GL2R in this setting. JOLT closes 89.6%–99.4% of that gap, and the rounded vocabularies end up only 0.008%–0.176% above the LP bound. So the small margin over BPE is not a rounding artifact; it reflects genuinely limited headroom.

From a pretraining perspective, two caveats matter. First, the paper studies token-count compression, not LM perplexity or downstream accuracy, so the results should not be read as direct evidence of better model quality. Second, the setup is byte-level with single-byte fallback tokens, so there is no OOV issue; the conclusions are specifically about the compression ceiling of GL2R-aware vocabulary optimization under that regime.11:T432,DataOrchestra combines an orchestrator with downstream tool models. Given a text chunk, the orchestrator first chooses among drop, untouch, and clean. For examples to be cleaned, it selects a subset of processing stages and generates example-specific instructions for LLM rewriting stages. The stages include noise pruning for line-level boilerplate and ads, surface rectification for formatting and grammar repairs, and pedagogical augmentation for math, Wikipedia-like, or reasoning-heavy content. Training uses a teacher LLM to propose initial plans, executes those plans with the actual tool models, and then evolves the plans using execution feedback. Stages that make minimal changes or harm the original content are removed; instructions are refined when the output loses information, introduces factual errors, or leaves residual noise. This produces 300K training pairs for fine-tuning the orchestrator. The important design choice is grounding the policy in the observed behavior of the tool models, rather than relying only on a teacher model’s high-level plan.12:T429,The paper proposes a five-level Data Pyramid: real-robot data, UMI-style data, egocentric and exocentric human interaction data, simulation data, and general vision-language data. The two primary axes are scalability and robot alignment. Real-robot data is directly aligned with the platform that produced it and has high physical fidelity, but it is expensive to collect and reset. UMI-style data removes the robot from the collection loop while retaining end-effector-centric manipulation signals, making it more scalable than robot demonstrations and more actionable than ordinary video. Human ego/exo video captures task structure, affordances, and hand-object interaction, but lacks robot-native action labels. Simulation provides controllable state-action trajectories, with transfer limited by assets, task coverage, and sim-to-real gaps. General vision-language data supplies broad semantic and perceptual priors but weak physical and action supervision. The paper further characterizes each source by quality, diversity, reusability, and physical fidelity.13:T4bc,The post is a position statement rather than a technical contribution, but its policy ranking is precise. First, restrict access to advanced chips and chipmaking equipment for authoritarian states, and crack down on smuggling and evasion, because scaling laws imply that without sufficient chips it is not possible to train models beyond the U.S. frontier. Second, target industrial-scale distillation, because distillation is far more compute-efficient than training from scratch and can compress the capability gap to a few months even under chip constraints; in the author's view, this matters much more than whether weights are open or closed. Third, require mandatory pre-release safety testing for all sufficiently capable models, open or closed, regardless of origin, with emphasis on cyber, biological, and alignment risks. The author argues that banning U.S. companies from using Chinese open weights does not address misuse, because malicious actors are unlikely to be regulated U.S. businesses. Open weights do introduce unique safety problems—weak guardrail enforcement, limited monitoring, and irreversible release—but the proposed response is capability-based testing rather than a blanket ban.14:T7d9,技术路线可以拆成四块。第一块是长上下文与深层信息流。Kimi Delta Attention 负责长序列混合，周期性插入 Gated MLA 层保留全局交互；这说明他们没有完全放弃 attention，而是用更便宜的序列混合主干配合稀疏的全局 attention 校正。Attention Residuals 则让每层可以选择性访问所有前层表示，缓解超深网络中的信息衰减与优化困难。第二块是超大规模稀疏 MoE。K3 使用 Stable LatentMoE，把 routed expert 空间扩到 896 个，每个 token 激活 16 个 expert，外加 shared expert。这里的难点不是 MoE 结构本身，而是极高稀疏度下的训练稳定性与负载均衡。报告提到用 normalization、SiTU-GLU 和 Quantile Balancing 来稳定路由与激活分布，目标是既扩大专家容量，又避免 router collapse、负载失衡和训练震荡。第三块是训练与数据 recipe。公开文本里没有把所有数据细节完全展开，但明确把 refined data and training recipes 作为 2.5× scaling efficiency 的组成部分，而不是把收益全部归因于架构。这一点很重要，因为大模型报告常把数据因素弱化。第四块是 post-training 与基础设施协同。RL 覆盖 general、agentic、coding 和多种 reasoning effort level，训练环境包括可验证搜索、专业知识工作、软件工程、kernel optimization、vision-in-the-loop 工具使用、persistent assistant workflow 等。不同领域和 effort 的策略最后通过 multi-teacher on-policy distillation 合并到统一模型。系统侧则分别为 KDA、2.8T MoE 预训练、百万 token agentic RL 和 serving 做了专门设计：如 fused kernels、KDA context parallelism、state-aware prefix caching、MoonEP 的静态 shape 与零拷贝通信、外部 KV 保留、partial rollout、可恢复 microVM sandbox 等。这里最值得注意的是，他们把“能否训练和运行长轨迹 RL”当成模型设计的一部分，而不是后处理问题。15:T848,The technical design has four major pieces. First is long-context and deep information flow. Kimi Delta Attention serves as the main long-sequence mixing mechanism, while Gated MLA layers are periodically interleaved to preserve global interaction. This suggests they do not fully abandon attention; instead they use a cheaper sequence-mixing backbone plus sparse global correction. Attention Residuals let each layer selectively access representations from all previous layers, which is meant to reduce information decay and optimization difficulty in very deep networks. Second is ultra-large sparse MoE. K3 uses Stable LatentMoE with 896 routed experts and 16 activated experts per token, plus shared experts. The challenge here is not the MoE idea itself but stable optimization and balanced routing under extreme sparsity. The report highlights normalization, SiTU-GLU, and Quantile Balancing as the stabilizers that keep routing and activation distributions under control. Third is the training and data recipe. The public text does not fully expose all data details, but it explicitly includes refined data and training recipes as part of the claimed efficiency gain rather than attributing everything to architecture. That is an important signal. Fourth is post-training and infrastructure co-design. RL spans general reasoning, agentic tasks, coding, and multiple reasoning-effort levels. Training environments include verifiable search, professional knowledge work, software engineering, kernel optimization, multimodal tool use, persistent assistant workflows, and autonomous execution. These specialized policies are later merged through multi-teacher on-policy distillation. On the systems side, the report introduces fused kernels, KDA context parallelism, state-aware prefix caching, MoonEP for perfectly balanced expert execution with static shapes and zero-copy communication, plus external KV retention, partial rollouts, and resumable microVM sandboxes for million-token agentic RL. A notable design choice is that long-horizon RL support is treated as part of the model stack, not as an afterthought.16:T556,The key reported scales are 2.8T total parameters, 104B activated parameters, 1M-token context, and native vision capability. The headline claim is an approximately 2.5x improvement in overall scaling efficiency over Kimi K2. This is not a single benchmark delta; it is presented as a combined effect of architecture, MoE stabilization, and refined data/training recipes. Evaluation spans long-horizon coding, agentic tasks, knowledge, reasoning, and vision. In the authors’ benchmark suite, Kimi K3 trails the strongest proprietary systems, Claude Fable 5 and GPT-5.6 Sol, but is reported to be consistently ahead of the other open and proprietary models included there. For pretraining researchers, the more important signals are structural: 16-of-896 sparse activation appears trainable at 104B active scale; 1M context is designed jointly with architecture and RL trajectory length rather than treated as pure inference extrapolation; and multi-effort RL is integrated into the unified model rather than exposed as a separate specialist policy. If the full report contains detailed ablations, the most valuable ones to inspect are the KDA versus Gated MLA mix, the effect of Attention Residuals on optimization at depth, the load and loss behavior of Stable LatentMoE versus standard top-k MoE, and the contribution of data recipe changes to the scaling curve.17:T462,Read it along three tracks. First, study the architecture overview and block diagrams to understand how KDA, Gated MLA, Attention Residuals, and Stable LatentMoE are composed, and why the design keeps all three forms of information flow: along sequence length, across depth, and across expert width. Second, read the pretraining and systems sections carefully, especially the details around MoE stability, routing balance, parallelism, MoonEP, and communication design. That is where much of the practical value lies for anyone training very large sparse models. Third, read the post-training and RL infrastructure sections to see how million-token trajectories are made feasible and how multi-effort policies are consolidated. Prioritize figures on scaling efficiency, long-context performance, MoE load statistics, and RL gains by task family. If time is limited, start with the introduction, architecture overview, and pretraining/systems sections, then sample the RL consolidation part. A full careful read likely takes 1.5 to 2.5 hours because this is a large-stack technical report rather than a narrow method paper.18:T449,Multi-Block Editing adds a bounded revision phase to block diffusion. At inference time, after a new block is decoded, the method reopens a sliding window of already decoded blocks and performs joint forward passes over that window, allowing earlier tokens to be revised using later context. The design avoids token-level rollback logic and keeps the computation bounded by editing whole blocks through direct token-to-token revision. For windows beyond adjacent blocks, an entropy-based historical surprisal watermark is used to select the previously decoded block that is most likely to contain errors, rather than recomputing all history. The training component, Multi-Block Edit SFT, introduces bidirectional attention masks and a curriculum that expands the edit span from 2 to 4 blocks, so the model learns to exploit multi-block context instead of seeing it only at inference time. On the systems side, the authors extend SGLang with a multi-shape CUDA Graph pool and fine-grained KV-cache control to support interleaved single-block decoding and variable-length multi-block editing passes.19:T48e,PIVOT groups nearby queries and replaces multiple full-prefix scans with one shared scan. A group is aggregated into a single proxy query, which traverses the prefix once and selects a shared candidate set of size c. Then there are two variants. PIVOT-Refine rescoring: each query re-scores only the shared candidate set and picks its own top-k. This changes per-query work from O(L) to O(c), where k < c << L. PIVOT-Reuse is cheaper: it directly reuses the proxy top-k for all queries in the group, skipping per-query refinement. For a group of size g, indexing cost drops from O(gL) to O(L + gc) for Refine or O(L) for Reuse. The method relies on two empirical properties shown in the paper: high top-k overlap among nearby queries, and long-tailed indexer scores along the key axis, so a candidate budget only slightly above k is often enough. Importantly, PIVOT preserves the DSA interface and leaves the downstream Sparse MLA operator unchanged, making it a drop-in replacement. In prefill, groups are formed by contiguous query positions; in decode, the d+1 tokens from one multi-token prediction step form a natural group, so PIVOT composes with MTP speedups.1a:T959,方法上最重要的是把“语义写入”从普通上下文利用中剥离出来。作者把 agent 历史表示成事件序列，一次 global prefill 后，每个事件的 KV 都已经被其前文上下文化。随后 serving 只取一个子集，故意删掉真正包含答案的 source event，只保留一个 downstream root event；这个 root event 的文本只引用 source，例如“ M mirrors S ”，但不显式说出值。若此时模型仍能回答 M 的状态，而且答案随 donor pair 中被删 source 的值切换，就说明 root 的 KV 行中已经 materialize 了上游状态。

论文围绕三个维度建立了“memory contract”。第一是 trigger：什么样的表面构造能触发写入。作者做了 16 种构造 bank，发现语义相近并不够，表面形式差异会让 write-through 从接近随机到 0.95 不等，说明 materialization 不是抽象语义层面自动成立，而是强依赖 checkpoint 对特定句式和关系模式的内部编码。第二是 landing：写入后的状态主要落在哪些被保留行里。结果显示 root event 承载主导 donor-aligned 信号，而某些 reference edge 更多像路由器，帮助查询回到 root；同时单个 row 的可读出内容还依赖 co-served 上下文，说明这不是一个完全局部、上下文无关的存储单元。第三是 access：这个通道能承载多大 payload。作者发现它更像紧凑状态通道，而不是通用信息通道：二元状态恢复明显高于随机，但 4-way、8-way 多分类接近机会水平，三位数字 payload 基本读不出来。

另一个关键贡献是“programmable carrier”。作者不满足于被动观察自然 mention，而是构造一种刻意书写、但不直接泄露答案的 carrier event，用来把上游状态写进后续表示。这个 carrier 能把 donor-aligned recovery 从 6% 提到 51%；在固定 query 位置上，只是把这个 carrier 与共享 downstream row 一起服务，就能把读出从 0.00 提到 0.42。这里的含义很直接：如果系统知道某类状态在未来可能需要、但原始操作数会被 eviction，那么可以在写时显式制造一个“承载事件”来提高后续可读性。不过作者也很克制，指出自然文本中的被动 harvesting 并不是可靠接口，真正工程上稳妥的做法仍然是保留显式 textual record 作为 fallback。1b:T94f,The main methodological contribution is a clean causal probe for hidden state transfer through KV. The history is modeled as a sequence of events. After one global prefill, each event’s KV is contextualized by everything before it. Serving then keeps only a subset of event blocks, deliberately dropping the source event that contains the value of interest while retaining a downstream root event whose text refers to the source but does not restate the value. If the model can still answer the query and the answer flips with the omitted source value across donor pairs, the retained root KV has materialized upstream-derived state.

The paper organizes the phenomenon into a three-part contract: trigger, landing, and access. Trigger asks which surface constructions actually induce write-through. The authors build a 16-construction bank and show that semantic equivalence is not enough: constructions that are all easy to read can differ dramatically in whether they cause state to be written into downstream representations. This means materialization is sensitive to form, not just meaning, and must be calibrated per checkpoint. Landing asks where the donor-aligned signal resides once the source is gone. The dominant signal lands in the root event, while some served reference edges mostly help route the query back to that root. Importantly, what a row yields depends on what other spans are co-served with it, so the retained row is not a context-free storage cell. Access asks how much information this channel can carry. The answer is: only compact state. Binary payloads are recoverable well above chance, but larger discrete payloads collapse toward chance, and multi-digit values are absent.

A particularly useful part is the programmable carrier construction. Instead of passively harvesting naturally occurring mentions, the authors intentionally write an answer-free carrier event designed to commit upstream state into later representations without explicitly naming the value. This substantially improves donor-aligned recovery. The engineering message is concrete: if a system expects some state to be needed after its operands are evicted, it may be possible to write that state forward on purpose. But the paper is careful not to oversell this. Passive natural mentions are not a reliable interface, and explicit textual records remain the robust fallback.1c:T743,The headline result is the donor-pair test on Qwen3-8B. Among donor-sensitive items, the model’s answer follows the omitted source value at a 99:0 ratio, even though no served span states which value is correct. That is the central evidence that retained KV rows can expose hidden upstream-derived state independently of visible text.

The programmable-carrier experiment is the most actionable systems result. On Qwen3-8B, an intentionally written answer-free carrier raises donor-aligned recovery from 6% to 51%. In a fixed query-position setup, adding the carrier alongside a shared downstream row moves the readout from 0.00 to 0.42. This shows that materialization is not merely an accidental property of one token position; it can be strengthened by writing a better downstream carrier.

The trigger study uses a 16-construction bank. Average read correctness of the constructions is reported at 0.98, but write-through ranges from chance to 0.95, and no construction is consistently strong across all three recent models tested. This is an important negative result: understanding a relation in text does not imply that the model will commit the resulting state into later KV in a way that survives source eviction.

The access-boundary experiments are also informative. Binary state recovers at 0.934 versus a 0.5 chance baseline, but 4-way and 8-way payloads sit near chance, and three-digit payloads are effectively absent. That sharply limits the interpretation of the phenomenon: it is a compact-state channel, not a general-purpose hidden memory bus.

Additional analyses on landing and co-serving show that the root event carries the dominant donor-aligned signal, while reference edges mostly help access it. The recoverability of a row also depends on which other spans are served together, which matters directly for eviction policy design.1d:T5f5,Start with the setup, not the claims. Read the introduction and Fig. 1 carefully until the donor-pair comparison is fully clear. The key object is the independently served KV block of a downstream event, not the visible text span itself. Then read Section 3, where the 99:0 result is established and the paper explains how it avoids confounds such as explicit answer leakage, priors, and decoy elimination. The note about independently sampled decoys is worth attention because many memory-style evaluations quietly fail there.

After that, Sections 4 to 6 are the core. Section 4 is about trigger sensitivity and is the fastest way to understand why this is not a stable semantic API. Section 5 is the most relevant part for systems and memory-policy work because it studies where the signal lands and how co-serving changes what can be read out. Section 6 gives the capacity boundary, which is essential if you want to judge whether the mechanism is practically usable.

If time is limited, read the figures and conclusion paragraphs of Sections 3–6 first, then return to the methodological details. Section 7 is the engineering takeaway: programmable carriers versus passive harvesting. Appendix F is worth a quick pass because it explains why “dropping the source causes no loss” is not evidence that the source was unnecessary. A useful skim takes about 30–45 minutes; if you work on KV memory, long-horizon agents, or cache eviction, the donor-pair design and the access-boundary analysis deserve a closer read.1e:T4ec,1) Estimation framing: each cached item x has latent utility u(x), roughly how much keeping it reduces future loss; an eviction policy is an estimator û(x).
2) Commit lag H as the key degree of freedom:
- H=0 covers existing approaches that decide at arrival: filtering (past-only signals like recency or accumulated attention) and prediction (learned guesses about future reuse).
- H→∞ corresponds to Belady’s offline optimum.
3) Fixed-lag smoothing: delay the keep/evict commitment by H steps so near-future evidence of reuse is observed rather than predicted, trading off buffer memory and latency.
4) Belady bridge via demonstrated utility: since neural attention lacks an explicit request stream, the paper defines a proxy “request” signal from which cached items were actually used by attention in correct near-future predictions.
5) Resulting policy: RMM, a training-free eviction rule that strictly generalizes H2O; additionally, a small model is trained to predict an ordering that is then measured/confirmed under the lag.
6) Regime map: when reuse is endogenous and temporally separated, smoothing can help; for common single-shot long-context reading, the measurement collapses to accumulated attention, making RMM effectively H2O-like.1f:T493,XIH-Bench is built to isolate language as the independent variable: keep task semantics and conflict structure fixed, then systematically swap the languages used in the higher- vs lower-priority instructions. The benchmark spans 6 languages (EN/DE/ES/FR/HI/ZH), 4 conflict domains, and 3 hierarchy settings (System>>User, System>>Tool, User>>Tool). Each instance has a Reference condition (only the higher-priority instruction) and a Conflict condition (an explicit contradictory lower-priority instruction); the performance drop from Reference to Conflict quantifies hierarchy breakage. Two consistent findings are reported: (1) language-dependent asymmetry across hierarchy positions (a language that boosts following at the top can be disruptive at the bottom), and (2) a Language Boundary Effect where cross-language conflicts yield higher compliance than same-language conflicts, plausibly because language contrast provides an extra boundary cue for separating instruction sources. The paper also attributes part of the variance to model-specific language specialization, where a favored language exerts disproportionate influence even when it should be overridden.20:T577,The contribution is an evaluation framework.
- Key reframing: attribution should be judged at the knowledge/support level, not merely by output sensitivity. Under IW/ICL overlap, sensitivity is not identifiable: removing a supporting span may not change the output because the same fact is available in weights.
- Metrics to expose this confounding:
  - BCS (Base-model Context attribution Score): evaluate attribution using a base model intended to have less IW access to the target knowledge, creating a control condition.
  - CAC (Cross-model Attribution Consistency): measure how attribution changes across models with different IW knowledge states; large, systematic drift indicates the method is reacting to IW overlap rather than contextual support.
  - APS (Attribution Preservation Score): quantify whether supporting spans keep their attribution when IW availability changes.
  - SSP (Source Separation Precision): treat the task as explicit source separation (ICL vs IW) and measure precision directly.
- Dataset: WMDP-Cyber++ adds provenance-style ground truth labels over context segments (which spans are truly supporting vs irrelevant, and which are IW-overlapping), enabling systematic evaluation.
The paper evaluates several representative methods (ContextCite, AttriBot, TracLLM, Token-Shapley) and argues the failure is structural for removal-based approaches, not a tuning issue.21:T457,The framework decomposes deep-search behavior into two dimensions. Search Decision-Making covers evidence sufficiency judgment, whether to invoke search, and how to decompose or reformulate a query. Information Synthesis & Verification covers authority, timeliness, cross-source agreement, inconsistency detection, multi-hop evidence integration, and rejection of plausible distractors. The benchmark construction uses document-grounded reverse engineering. It starts from high-quality real-world documents as complete evidential hindsight, then works backward to synthesize a natural query, answer, localized supporting evidence, and reasoning structure. It keeps topically related documents as natural noise and builds evidence-anchored distractors by perturbing authority, publication time, entity, or version. Multi-step filtering is used to check answerability, non-memorability, evidence-label correctness, and adversarial validity. Unlike query-first retrieval benchmarks, the answer and evidence structure are fixed before task construction, so document composition becomes an explicit control variable.22:T5ca,The core design is AcquaBench, built around matched CLEAN, GOLD, and SHAM conditions. CLEAN preserves only the benchmark-authorized information state. GOLD keeps the same qid, model, action channel, reference target, and scorer, but adds a controlled source that makes the correct target value available. SHAM preserves the same source structure and exposure opportunity as GOLD, while substituting a matched incorrect value. This is the key move: the paper does not merely ask whether the agent was exposed to a source, but whether success tracks the correctness of the supplied value under a matched intervention. Two contrasts are defined. Delta_GC measures the total score response when the correct target becomes available. Delta_GS measures the correct-versus-incorrect value substitution effect under the same exposure structure. The latter is the central provenance quantity because it isolates value dependence from generic source effects. The intervention is instantiated on four standardized surfaces: retrieval, tool, memory, and subagent messaging. The paper also emphasizes joint qid-clustered analysis rather than treating these surfaces as independent replications. A notable part of the methodology is the boundary analysis: when answer sufficiency is distributed across multiple sources, simple single-source exposure markers can fail, so the authors explicitly test whether provenance effects survive beyond the intended observation unit of a single-source probe.23:T46a,Three findings carry most of the weight. First, under direct single-source acquisition, GOLD exceeds SHAM by 19.1 to 25.9 percentage points across all three tested models. This gap survives a style control, which is important because it argues against the effect being driven by formatting or wording artifacts. Second, under distributed answer sufficiency, where the answer-supporting information is split across two sources, GOLD still beats SHAM by 11.8 and 14.6 points. At the same time, single-source coloc no longer works as a useful marker of earned success, with AUROC dropping to 0.376 and 0.142. That result is conceptually important: source exposure evidence is not the same as evidence that success depended on the correct target value. Third, provenance auditing changes model comparison. The paper reports a supported CLEAN advantage of 5.0 points for Qwen2.5-32B over Qwen2.5-14B, but this compresses to a raw GOLD difference of -0.6 points. The authors are careful not to overclaim rank inversion, yet the result is enough to show that uncontrolled information conditions can obscure or distort supported ordering.24:T457,CADCON is a five-feature binary design-intent header (CIRCLE, NGON, THIN, TALL, MULTI_PART) prepended to CadQuery-style sketch-extrude programs (transpiled from DeepCAD histories). Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B is LoRA-fine-tuned under this conditioning. Two methodological innovations carry the study: (1) Metric independence—adherence is re-scored by executable geometric assertions on the produced B-rep solid, sharing no code with the training-side regex extractor used to build header labels. The full evaluation matrix ({0%, 40%} prefix × {correct, wrong, masked} header × 3 seeds × both tokenizations) and all decision rules are pre-registered before scoring. (2) Causal control—a derangement-trained control group receives shuffled ground-truth headers (each program gets another program's header), so the header marginal distribution is byte-identical to standard training but the header↔program correlation is destroyed. If the control shows no performance drop under wrong headers, the harm can be attributed to the learned header→program mapping rather than a mechanical distribution-shift confound.25:T509,Four key findings: (1) A semantically wrong header hurts more than no header. In the 40%-prefix conditional completion regime, wrong headers degrade geometric adherence from 0.425 to 0.305 (text headers) / 0.215 (token headers), direction-unanimous across three seeds and both tokenizations (token-side p ≤ 8e-4 per seed, ~1e-5 for two seeds). The effect survives on the valid-geometry subset, ruling out a validity artifact. (2) The harm requires the learned header→program mapping. The derangement-trained control remains competent but shows no correct→wrong drop, while the standard model drops sharply. The control produces identical geometric feature sets under correct and wrong headers on 71–98% of jointly-executable outputs, versus 35–43% for the standard model. The interaction is significant on 3/3 seeds (p = 4.2e-3 / 6.7e-6 / 3.6e-6). (3) The independent metric deflates conditioning benefit. The apparent gain of a correct header shrinks substantially relative to the regex metric (text +31% vs +52% relative; token +4% vs +46%), quantifying metric circularity in conditioning claims. (4) The harm is regime-specific. At 0% prefix the unconditional baseline cannot produce valid CAD at all (0/100 executable), so "wrong < none" is undefined and does not hold there.26:T6f3,The paper first diagnoses the mechanism, then proposes a training-free fix. The diagnosis centers on the claim that standard dual-encoder similarity s(I,T)=<f(I),g(T)> behaves like soft pooling over atomic concept evidence rather than logical execution. Using one- and two-concept queries on COCO, images are partitioned into four quadrants: Both, A-only, B-only, and Neither. The authors then inspect mean scores and rankings under different logical forms. Anti-monotone queries such as ¬A∧¬B systematically prefer the Both quadrant, which should be the least preferred one. This shows that negation does not actually alter the ranking mechanism. The paper further analyzes the text embedding into dominant concept-related directions and weaker operator-related residuals, arguing that operator information exists but is attenuated or poorly aligned with image evidence under dot-product scoring.

Based on that diagnosis, the paper proposes factored inference and LCSE. The idea is to separate evidence extraction from constraint execution. A frozen VLM is used to score each atomic concept independently, producing concept-level evidence. Boolean operators are then executed outside the model using the parsed query structure. To avoid discarding useful non-logical information captured by the original similarity score, LCSE performs logic-constrained score editing rather than replacing the score entirely. It adaptively edits the holistic similarity only when the query requires non-mean aggregation. This preserves standard retrieval behavior where no logical constraint is present. The trade-off is clear: no retraining and better semantic control, at the cost of requiring query parsing and currently focusing on Boolean compositions over one or two visual concepts.27:T549,The experiments have three main parts. First, the paper shows on COCO val2017 that CLIP-like models can detect whether a concept is present, yet fail to execute negation in ranking. A representative result reported in the text is that concept detection reaches about AUC 0.88, while the rankings induced by “a X” and “no X” have Spearman correlation around 0.94. So the model changes the text embedding, but not enough to change the ordering. Second, the paper introduces FACTOR-Bench, built from operator-contrastive caption pairs with validated concept presence and absence labels, to test whether a model follows Boolean semantics rather than merely tracking mentioned concepts. Third, LCSE is evaluated on FACTOR-Bench, NegBench, and retrieval-retention settings against standard CLIP, SigLIP, and fine-tuned variants. The reported pattern is that LCSE improves constraint-sensitive retrieval while preserving ordinary retrieval better than training-based fixes. The paper also argues that fine-tuning on negation-heavy data often harms standard retrieval because it tries to repair an interface problem at the representation level.

The excerpt here supports the main qualitative findings and a few concrete numbers, but not the full result tables. Exact benchmark gains should be taken from Section 5 and the appendix rather than inferred.28:T564,1. Gating suppression: across five injection designs (attention biases, gated prefix tokens, margin-trained compositional objectives, etc.), even when gate parameters nominally pass 30–45% of the signal, ablating the pathway at inference leaves task accuracy unchanged — pathways are behaviorally closed. Two regimes are identified: (a) dead-gradient regime, formalized in Proposition 1, where caption-invariance of image-derived signals nulls first-order discriminative gradients; (b) negative-utility regime, where the auxiliary signal actively harms the loss and gradients consistently drive the gate toward zero. 2. Geometric regularization: hierarchical visual scene relations are encoded as a continuous latent graph on the Lorentz hyperboloid, with IoA-driven entailment loss and angular repulsion loss acting as auxiliary losses during LoRA training. The geometric computation only participates in the forward pass at training time and can be dropped entirely at inference, constraining LoRA from drifting away from the pretrained manifold via forward coupling. 3. Prefix injection stability: prefix tokens projected from hyperbolic space exhibit norm mismatch with text embeddings; RMS normalization of prefixes to the text embedding distribution resolves generation collapse, which is attributed to residual-stream dominance rather than attention-softmax saturation.29:T475,1. Spatially-Grounded Thought Tokenization: the image is partitioned into an N×N grid of regions, each thought token corresponds to an explicit spatial location. The MLLM generates these tokens within the reasoning chain, compressing visual and semantic clues into interpretable latent representations; a decoder then reconstructs target regions from these spatially-aligned thought tokens. 2. Context-Adaptive Chain-of-Tokens: a three-stage Scan-Focus-Action paradigm dynamically switches grounding modes: Perceptual Scanning generates Perceptual Thought Tokens (P-Tokens) to capture fine-grained visual cues; a Switch Adapter evaluates aggregated context, decoding P-Tokens directly for high-confidence perceptual grounding, otherwise entering Contextual Re-focusing to inject textual reflection clues and visual evidence from candidate regions; the final Decisive Action stage generates Reasoning Thought Tokens (R-Tokens) fused with P-Tokens for reasoning-driven grounding. 3. PR-Bench is constructed to disentangle referring expressions into perception and reasoning dimensions for quantifying the perception-reasoning performance gap.2a:T45c,OmniVAE is a jointly trained audio-video VAE framework explicitly designed for cross-modal latent space alignment, retaining separate audio and video encoder-decoder branches (preserving per-modality independent reconstruction capability, with no extra inference cost). Beyond standard reconstruction losses, two training-only objectives are introduced: 1) Uni-modal semantic distillation: Uses frozen pretrained Qwen3-Omni vision and audio encoders to supervise the latent representations of the video and audio branches respectively, injecting semantic structure into each modality's latent space to improve learnability of the representations. 2) Segment-level audio-video contrastive learning: Applies spatial aggregation to video latent frames to obtain token sequences, then aggregates audio and video latents into matched equal-length temporal segments, and computes bidirectional InfoNCE loss in a shared projection space to achieve cross-modal alignment at fine temporal granularity. Both objectives are only activated during training; the two VAEs run independently at inference with no additional latency.2b:T426,Two desirable properties for group-based RL optimizers are first formalized: (P1) Gradient unbiasedness — the gradient estimator is an unbiased estimate of the true policy gradient; (P2) Length invariance — each trajectory's total contribution to gradient updates is independent of its length. An impossibility theorem is then proven: under outcome rewards (scalar reward assigned only at generation end, e.g. 0/1 for correctness) and group-relative baselines, no length-based weighting function can satisfy both P1 and P2 simultaneously. A parametric family of weighting functions f_α(L) = L^(α-1) (α ∈ [0,1]) characterizes the full tradeoff spectrum: α=0 recovers GRPO (length-normalized, length-invariant but gradient-biased), α=1 recovers Dr. GRPO (no length normalization, gradient-unbiased but length-biased). Quantitative analysis shows that for two trajectories with length ratio r, the longer trajectory captures r/(1+r) of the total gradient signal under Dr. GRPO, meaning length bias can be substantial even for moderate length differences.2c:T46f,The work packages three components:
1) SoMBench: a psychology-grounded benchmark that defines social intelligence as a structured capability space (3 primary dimensions, 17 secondary dimensions, 71 task paradigms). It uses shared scenarios (284) and controls factors like question format, narrative perspective, and context length to reduce confounds.
2) Zing training recipe: capability diagnosis is used to construct targeted supervision, followed by SFT, on-policy distillation to better match interactive distributions, and rubric-based reinforcement learning to strengthen social reasoning and behavior.
3) Actio deployment harness: a harness-controlled architecture that routes typed supports into inference—procedural guidance, runtime mental-state representations, reusable experience, and external social/normative knowledge—explicitly offloading part of the “social priors” from parameters to structured retrieval/tools/memory.
The key design choice is to treat social intelligence as a layered capability with measurement, parametric internalization, and runtime grounding, rather than as a single dataset or reward.2d:T426,Its value is not just another latency-reduction trick for reasoning models. It reframes a deeper issue: are reasoning tokens merely a training scaffold, or are they an indispensable inference-time computational medium? The answer here is conditional rather than absolute. The paper shows that internalizability depends on the task and on the generalization regime. On easier or more structured settings, a student can absorb a meaningful fraction of the teacher’s reasoning signal into parameters. On harder math OOD settings, removing explicit reasoning hurts more, and partial scaffolding recovers a substantial part of the gap. That matters for work on RL-trained reasoning models, test-time compute, implicit CoT, and latent reasoning, because it turns the debate into a measurable performance-cost trade-off. Another useful contribution is the comparison between masked distillation and plain SFT on teacher rollouts: the training objective itself affects whether the student learns compressed internal computation or merely imitates surface trajectories.2e:T8dc,The core method is masked distillation. During training, the teacher operates in thinking mode and has access to the question q, the full reasoning trace r, and the answer a. The student only sees q and is trained to generate answer tokens without access to the intermediate reasoning tokens. The key point is that this is not simple hard-label SFT. The teacher, conditioned on q+r, provides token-level supervision over the student’s answer tokens. So the student is trained to match the teacher’s answer distribution under asymmetric conditioning: the teacher has the CoT, the student does not. This forces the student to compress information that was explicit in the teacher’s context into its own parameters.

The paper instantiates this in two settings. In self-distillation, the same model serves as both a thinking teacher and a non-thinking student, isolating whether explicit reasoning can be folded back into parameters within the same architecture family. In dual-model distillation, a reasoning model supervises a separate non-thinking student, which is closer to a practical deployment setup with a strong expensive teacher and a cheaper student.

A second important design is α-suffix masked distillation. Instead of treating reasoning as a binary choice, the teacher trace is split into a prefix and a suffix. The student is trained to internalize the first (1-α) portion and, at inference time, explicitly emits only the remaining α suffix together with the final answer. α=0 means direct-answer generation with no visible reasoning; α=1 approximates full explicit reasoning. This is a strong experimental design choice because it turns the role of reasoning tokens into a continuous variable and lets the paper trace out an explicit accuracy-cost frontier.

The paper also studies the objective itself. A key baseline is whether one can simply SFT on teacher rollouts and obtain similar benefits. The evidence argues that masked distillation is meaningfully different: the point is not to reproduce the teacher trajectory, but to align the student’s answer distribution when the student is missing the teacher’s intermediate context. That distinction matters if one cares about internalized computation rather than trajectory imitation.2f:T759,The experiments cover two domains: Math and Countdown, with explicit in-distribution and OOD evaluation. In Math, GSM8K is the main domain, while MATH-500 and AIME-25 serve as harder OOD splits. In Countdown, the paper introduces target-range and search-depth shifts to probe changes in combinatorial search difficulty. Both self-distillation and dual-model settings are evaluated. The scaffold parameter is swept over α∈{0, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0}, so the main result is a curve rather than a single operating point.

Three findings stand out. First, full internalization is not universal. On easier or more regular in-distribution tasks, α=0 direct-answer students can approach the teacher, indicating that a nontrivial fraction of CoT information can indeed be absorbed into parameters. On harder math OOD settings, removing explicit reasoning causes a clearer drop. Second, partial scaffolding works well. As α increases from 0 to intermediate values, students often recover a large fraction of the lost accuracy while using far fewer reasoning tokens than full-CoT inference. This suggests that a short visible suffix can compensate for the part of computation that does not compress well. Third, OOD performance depends more on explicit scaffolding than ID performance does. In other words, visible reasoning tokens appear to function not only as a training aid but also as a more robust channel for test-time computation under distribution shift.

The provided text does not include the full result tables, so I will not invent exact numbers. But the experimental structure is clear: the paper compares final accuracy, explicit reasoning length, self- vs dual-model distillation, masked distillation vs SFT baselines, and OOD degradation patterns. For a researcher, the most informative object is the shape of the accuracy-vs-α curve, not the best single benchmark number.30:T43f,For a skim, focus on three parts.

First, read the introduction carefully, especially the four research questions and the framing around whether reasoning tokens are necessary at inference time. That section states the paper’s actual contribution more clearly than the title alone.

Second, read the method section where masked distillation and the α-suffix variant are defined. Pay attention to what the teacher sees, what the student sees, where the loss is applied, and how this differs from plain SFT on teacher rollouts. That is the conceptual center of the paper.

Third, in the experiments, prioritize the main plots or tables that sweep α and separate ID from OOD. Do not only look at the top-line accuracy. Track three quantities together: answer accuracy, visible reasoning length, and OOD degradation. If time permits, also inspect the Countdown analysis, since it offers a cleaner controlled setting for understanding which computations are easier to internalize.

A useful skim should take about 30-45 minutes. If you only read one figure, read the main α-sweep result.31:T8c1,The method, StaIF, is a stage-wise optimization framework rather than a single unified objective. The paper splits constraints into two categories. Soft constraints are subjective and preference-like, such as tone, style, or encouraging phrasing. Hard constraints are objective and programmatically checkable, such as valid JSON, required fields, word-count limits, language requirements, or explicit formatting rules.

Stage 1 uses preference optimization to learn soft constraints. A notable design choice is the use of multiple negative responses for the same instruction rather than a single chosen/rejected pair. This matters because failures on complex instructions are often partial: a response may satisfy some constraints while missing others, or may be semantically correct but stylistically off. Multiple negatives expose the model to a richer set of near-miss errors and sharpen its sensitivity to soft-constraint violations.

Stage 2 applies reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards, implemented with GRPO. The key idea is to optimize hard constraints directly using deterministic verifiers instead of relying on a learned reward model or a holistic preference objective. For complex instruction following, this is a natural fit because many important failures are discrete and checkable.

On the data side, the paper proposes an automated synthesis pipeline and builds StaInstruct. The construction is not just random constraint concatenation. It explicitly models task-constraint compatibility and filters invalid, redundant, or conflicting constraints. Each example includes a complex instruction, a high-quality positive response, multiple negative responses targeted at soft constraints, and rule-based verification code for hard constraints. This data structure aligns cleanly with the two training stages: preference learning in Stage 1 and verifier-based RL in Stage 2.

Compared with standard DPO or single-stage RL, the novelty is less about a new optimization formula and more about decomposing the training signal to match the structure of the task. The paper’s claim is that a holistic objective tends to optimize overall acceptability, while stage-wise training improves strict per-constraint satisfaction.32:T666,The evaluation is conducted on three instruction-following benchmarks: IFEval, IFBench, and CFBench. Two open-source base models are used: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Tulu3-8B-SFT. The paper reports consistent gains across these bases and benchmarks, and states that StaIF-trained Tulu3-8B-SFT becomes competitive with some much larger proprietary models on IFEval and IFBench. Since the provided text does not include the full result tables, I will not fabricate exact scores, but the experimental structure is clear.

First, the main results show that the two-stage setup outperforms single-stage preference optimization and other baselines across multiple benchmarks, not just on one dataset. Second, the ablations indicate that both stages matter: Stage 1 improves soft-constraint sensitivity, while adding Stage 2 further strengthens strict compliance on verifiable constraints. Third, the multiple-negative design is empirically useful. The paper includes negative-sampling experiments showing that multiple rejected responses provide a stronger contrastive signal than a single negative. Fourth, the authors emphasize generalization rather than template memorization, suggesting that they analyze whether the model transfers to unseen combinations of constraints rather than merely fitting the training patterns.

For a researcher, the most interesting part is not the leaderboard position but the error breakdown by constraint type: which constraints are under-optimized by DPO-like objectives, which ones respond most to verifier-based RL, and whether the bilingual setup yields extra gains for Chinese complex instruction following.33:T474,A good skim order is: problem formulation, data construction, the two training stages, then ablations.

Start with the introduction and make sure the soft-vs-hard constraint split is convincing to you. The rest of the paper depends on that framing.

Then read the StaInstruct section carefully. It is arguably more important than the optimization details, because the ceiling on complex instruction following depends heavily on whether the training data really covers multi-constraint composition, conflict filtering, negative diversity, and verifier quality. Pay special attention to task-constraint compatibility, conflict filtering, and how verifier code is generated and validated.

After that, read the Stage 1 and Stage 2 training details, especially how multiple negatives are organized and how GRPO rewards are defined. Finally, inspect the ablations: single-stage vs two-stage, single-negative vs multiple-negatives, and performance broken down by benchmark or constraint type.

A useful skim should take about 25-35 minutes. If time is short, prioritize the data construction figure, the main result table, and the ablation table.34:T7be,The method learns a vector δ over the vocabulary and adds it to the base model logits at every step, yielding πδ(yt|x,y<t)=softmax(l(x,y<t)+δ). Because δ is context-independent, it can only induce a global reweighting of token preferences; it cannot implement prefix-specific decisions. The theory starts from a KL-regularized RL objective with terminal reward r(x,y1:T). If arbitrary policies are allowed, the optimum is an exponential tilt of the base policy, equivalent to a prefix-dependent logit correction derived from soft value or advantage terms. The proposed fixed-bias policy is then treated as a restricted approximation to that optimum. The paper derives a reward-gap bound showing that the approximation error depends on how much the optimal correction varies across prefixes. In plain terms, the method should work when the desired correction is mostly a stable token-level preference, such as favoring EOS, concise endings, or formatting tokens, and should fail when the correction must depend strongly on local context.

On the estimation side, the paper derives a closed-form IPS-style estimator that uses black-box rollouts, sequence-level rewards, and token probabilities. No backpropagation through the model is needed. Operationally, the estimator compresses systematic token-level differences between high-reward and low-reward trajectories into one shared bias vector. The paper also compares full-vocabulary bias against more restricted parameterizations and argues that full-vocabulary control is more robust, because many useful behavior changes require coordinated reordering across groups of tokens rather than a few manually selected ones. Relative to prompt optimization, this consumes no context and acts directly on the output distribution. Relative to PEFT or fine-tuning, it is almost non-invasive. Relative to generic black-box RL, the policy class is much smaller, which improves stability and sample efficiency but also lowers the ceiling.35:T556,The experiments are organized around three questions. First, can a fixed logit-bias vector produce measurable gains at all? The paper reports improvements over the base model on math and reasoning benchmarks while using far fewer trainable parameters and much less compute than conventional fine-tuning. Second, where does the method help most? The empirical pattern is that it works better when the target behavior has a strong global token-bias component, such as verbosity control, formatting, stopping behavior, or small distribution shifts with recurring answer patterns. It helps less on tasks that require fine-grained conditional decisions. Third, do the empirical results match the theory? The ablations indicate that full-vocabulary bias is better than restricted token subsets, and that KL regularization plus the IPS estimator improves training stability. The setup appears to rely mainly on RLVR-style rewards, where prompts have verifiable answers and rewards are computed from correctness or related rule-based checks.

The excerpt provided here does not include the full result tables, so it would be unsafe to invent exact model sizes, dataset sizes, or improvement numbers. For a precise reading, the main tables and appendix should be checked for rollout budgets, API call counts, and the exact compute comparison against LoRA or full fine-tuning.36:T428,The method starts from a POMDP formulation of VLM interaction, where the history includes observations, actions, rewards, and the prefix tokens generated in the current turn. The paper derives token-wise and turn-wise RL objectives and argues that their policy-gradient surrogates have the same gradient structure; the main difference lies in how the advantage is estimated. Based on this, it proposes Hybrid Advantage Estimation, a linear combination of token-level and turn-level advantages. This lets the policy receive fine-grained guidance for language generation and turn-level guidance from intermediate environment feedback. The paper further shows that, with a suitable discount factor and learning target, one critic can estimate both token-wise and turn-wise values. HyGAE instantiates this idea as an actor-critic algorithm, using the hybrid advantage for actor updates and a unified value target for critic training. The bias-variance discussion is used to motivate why the hybrid estimator can be more stable than either single-granularity estimator.37:T736,CALM formulates controller-aware post-training as multi-task reinforcement learning over controller-induced MDPs. Each controller is implemented as an executable Python program in a code-as-controller style. The controller specifies the sequence of model calls, active roles, branching logic, aggregation, and verification steps. Training then samples from a distribution over controllers rather than optimizing for a single fixed protocol.

The central modeling assumption is compositionality. Different controllers may have different global structures, but they share recurring local modules such as CoT generation, verification, planning, critique, and related reasoning behaviors. If the model learns these modules well, it may generalize to unseen controller compositions instead of memorizing complete workflows. To study this, the paper explores four module-level training strategies under a turn-level GRPO objective: flat mixing of all turns, uniform weighting across module types, adaptive gradient-norm balancing, and single-role-per-step training. These variants are motivated by a real optimization issue: controllers differ in turn count and role composition, so some modules naturally dominate the gradient signal unless training explicitly compensates for that imbalance.

What makes the paper interesting is not merely that it uses multi-task RL, but that it treats controller structure as a source of reusable local behaviors and then asks how optimization should respect that structure. Once the goal is cross-controller transfer, module-level credit assignment and gradient balancing become first-order concerns. The evaluation setup is aligned with this hypothesis: the paper distinguishes transfer to held-out compositions built from familiar modules from broader controller shifts that alter role inventories or interfaces.38:T605,From the full introduction, the main empirical conclusion is clear: multi-controller training consistently outperforms single-controller baselines on transfer. Models trained on one native controller may do well on that workflow but degrade on unseen controller compositions, whereas CALM is more robust both on held-out compositions and on broader controller shifts. The experiments are conducted on mathematical reasoning tasks, which helps isolate controller generalization from large changes in task distribution.

The excerpt provided does not include the full result tables, so exact numbers are not available here. Still, the experimental design is already informative. The paper evaluates two distinct generalization regimes. First, it tests recombination: can the model handle new workflows assembled from familiar local reasoning primitives? Second, it tests stronger structural shift: does the shared policy remain effective when the set of roles or the interaction interface changes? It also compares the four module-level training strategies to understand whether simple mixing is enough or whether explicit balancing across modules improves transfer.

The most important thing to look for in the full experiments is not a single benchmark score, but the relationship between controller diversity during training and workflow transfer at deployment. In particular, the ablations should reveal whether weighting and gradient-balancing strategies prevent controllers with more turns or denser rewards from dominating optimization.39:T595,For a skim, start with the problem framing in the introduction. The motivation is straightforward and well stated: deployment uses many controllers, while training usually optimizes for one. Then move to the method section and focus on three items: how controllers are represented as executable programs, how modules are defined, and how the four module-level training strategies differ in practice. Those details determine whether CALM is really learning reusable components or just jointly fitting several workflows.

In the experiments, prioritize the two transfer settings: held-out controller compositions and broader controller shifts. The first tests compositional generalization; the second tests robustness under stronger structural change. Then read the ablations comparing flat mixing, uniform weighting, adaptive gradient balancing, and single-role-per-step updates. If the paper includes controller code examples, they are worth a quick look because they help judge whether the module decomposition is natural and whether the framework could extend to tool use or agent settings.

A useful skim should take about 25–35 minutes. If your focus is RL post-training, inference-time compute, or controller search, the method and evaluation design are the main value. If your focus is core pretraining, the takeaway is more conceptual: controller compatibility may be an important capability axis for future base models.3a:T56c,The experimental design is intentionally simple. Invisible reasoning is defined as consequential computation in internal representations during the forward pass without an interpretable trace in output tokens. The authors insert a fixed filler-token sequence before the model’s answer. The key control is that the same filler sequence is used for every problem, so it cannot encode information about a particular question or answer at the token level. In evaluation, few-shot examples include the question, an assistant-side filler span, and the ground-truth answer; the test query is also followed by an assistant-side filler prefill before the model generates the answer. The baseline uses the same setup without filler. The paper proposes three diagnostic criteria: performance improves with filler tokens; the improvement depends on filler content; and filler preferences differ across models. These criteria make it less plausible that the effect comes from ordinary semantic prompting, and more plausible that model-specific representations and attention dynamics are being used. The tasks are short and verifiable: 4-digit multiplication, 5-to-7-step nested arithmetic, and counting distinct assigned variables in code snippets. The authors are careful not to reduce the mechanism to “more compute” alone, since inserting tokens also changes positions and attention patterns.3b:T461,The study evaluates 13 frontier language models. Many models benefit from filler tokens on the synthetic reasoning tasks, with gains up to about 13 percentage points. The gain depends strongly on the filler sequence, and the best filler content varies across models. This model-specific variation suggests that the effect is tied to internal token representations, positional interactions, and learned computation patterns rather than to a shared human-readable meaning of the filler. A safety-relevant experiment adds a hidden modular arithmetic objective alongside the primary task. For Claude Opus 4.5, filler tokens allow the model to satisfy the hidden constraint without sacrificing primary-task accuracy, while the extra objective remains invisible in the output text. The training experiments are more mixed: RL on Qwen3-235B induces strong preferences over filler content, but the filler-token benefit does not persist robustly at test time; supervised fine-tuning also fails to transfer it. The capability appears to exist in current models, but making it stable through straightforward RL or SFT is not solved.3c:T4d4,The authors first introduce Layer-wise Compositional Divergence (LCD), a metric that measures per-layer representation separation between negated and affirmative captions. Combining LCD measurements with per-layer visual alignment scores, they identify a "compositional peak layer" where syntactic information is maximally preserved, and a stable "anchor layer" located before the sharp rise in visual alignment, confirming the representational collapse in final layers. Based on this observation, they propose PeakPatch, a lightweight correction system that keeps CLIP fully frozen and adds two small modules: an Embedding Correction Network (ECN) that uses cross-attention to extract negation signals from the compositional peak layer, anchors these signals to stable representations from the anchor layer, and predicts a deviation vector to inject lost syntax into final-layer embeddings; a Score Correction Network (SCN) that compares peak-layer representations, corrected embeddings, and image embeddings to output bounded scalar corrections for cosine similarity scores. The two modules are trained end-to-end, adding 5.2M parameters (3.5% of the CLIP backbone) while preserving the standard cosine similarity inference interface.3d:T40a,The work has three parts. First, it compares 12 pre-trained LLMs against a set of classical and learning-based HMM predictors over 75 HMM configurations and a range of context lengths. This behavioral comparison rules out a single universal explanation and narrows the plausible mechanisms to three ansatz classes: Linear n-gram, Non-linear n-gram, and Spectral. Second, it shows that Transformers can implement these classes in context. The unifying view expresses next-token probabilities as linear functions of nonlinear features computed from past observations, which covers finite-window predictors, nonlinear feature predictors, and spectral-style methods. A small Transformer trained on a single HMM is used to validate that a finite-window-like predictor can emerge naturally. Third, the paper introduces Principal Activations Probe, a layer-wise probing and intervention pipeline that finds low-dimensional algorithmic signals in the residual stream and then tests whether changing those signals causally changes predictions.3e:T42b,The work uses declarative, zero-exemplar in-context specifications to impose conceptual structures (ordered cycles, hierarchical trees) that are either congruent or conflicting with pretrained priors. Tests cover entity types with varying prior strength: strong cyclic concepts (weekdays, months, clock hours), weak cyclic priors (zodiac signs), non-cyclic concepts (musical notes), and arbitrary meaningless tokens. Entity representations are read from the residual stream at the last prompt token — the integrated state consumed by next-token computation, rather than states induced by structural queries — and geometry is measured via pairwise dissimilarity and representational similarity analysis (RSA). Key innovations include: demonstrating topology-level control (cycle vs. tree) rather than just within-shape shifts; using conflicting specifications to disentangle context vs. pretrained prior effects; and using activation patching (entity substitution) to verify the context-set geometry is causally operative in generation, not just a probe correlate.3f:T7c1,最核心的结果有四个。第一，单独用 answer correctness 这个不可部署的 oracle 信号，检测人工标注 unfaithfulness 的 AUROC 达到 0.696，超过所有专门设计的 behavioral detectors。作者进一步指出，69% 的标注 unfaithfulness 出现在 incorrect answers 上，这解释了为什么很多 detector 看起来“有点效果”，其实主要是在做 accuracy prediction。

第二，分层后出现两个 regime。在 complete-feature subset 上，correct-answer 子集 n=363，behavioral signals 对 faithful 与 post-hoc reasoning 有中等区分能力，AUROC 大约 0.63–0.67；而 benchmark 自带的 step-removal 指标甚至会稳定指向错误方向。incorrect-answer 子集 n=270，且承载了大多数 unfaithfulness，在这个子集上作者测试的所有行为信号都与随机猜测无显著差异。

第三，内部表征与行为表征不一致。在线性 probe 实验中，Llama-3.1-8B 的 incorrect-answer regime 可以被 hidden states 解码，nested held-out AUROC 0.67，selection-corrected permutation p≤0.03；Qwen-2.5-7B 则在 correct-answer regime 上可解码，p=0.014。更重要的是，两个 regime 之间 probe 不可迁移，说明没有检测到共享、同向的线性方向。这意味着“unfaithfulness”不是单一现象，而至少包含机制上不同的两类。

第四，关于合成数据。作者比较了 7 个开源模型上的 instructed answer-first prompting，以及 Turpin 等人式的 hint-induced rationalization。两者在各自构造数据上都能被内部检测到，但只有 hint-induced variant 能向人工标注数据迁移，而且迁移到的是 incorrect-answer regime，而不是它表面上更像的 correct-answer post-hoc regime。instructed rationalization 则对真实标注数据两个 regime 都不迁移。这对常见的“先合成一批不忠实 CoT 再训练/评测 detector”做法是直接提醒：合成分布可能学到的是错的东西。40:T78e,The paper makes three methodological moves. First, it audits the label semantics of FaithCoT-Bench and finds a mismatch between the benchmark documentation and the released data for the four-way faithful_type codes. The authors verify the correct interpretation using three independent checks: the relation between stored parsed final answers and gold labels, consistency with the benchmark paper’s own per-model accuracy numbers, and consistency with the released per-trace metrics. This data-level correction is important because the later regime analysis depends on it.

Second, the paper implements four families of behavioral detectors and evaluates them against the human binary unfaithfulness labels, rather than against derived benchmark metrics such as soft_faithfulness. The authors explicitly show that correlating a detector with soft_faithfulness is close to circular, because soft_faithfulness is itself a detector-like score. They also include premise-graph structure signals and counterfactual interventions with validated premise extraction, aiming to cover a broad set of black-box detection strategies.

Third, the authors stratify the problem by answer correctness. This yields a correct-answer regime, where the task is mainly to distinguish faithful reasoning from post-hoc rationalization, and an incorrect-answer regime, where the task is to distinguish honest error from unfaithful error. They then add hidden-state linear probes to test whether internal representations contain decodable signals even when behavior does not, and they compare two synthetic unfaithfulness constructions—instructed answer-first rationalization and hint-induced rationalization—to see whether either transfers to the human-annotated benchmark. That transfer test is a strong design choice: the question is not whether synthetic traces are detectable in isolation, but whether they share structure with real unfaithfulness.41:T837,There are four main empirical results. First, answer correctness alone—an oracle diagnostic, not a deployable detector—achieves AUROC 0.696 against human-annotated unfaithfulness, outperforming every purpose-built behavioral detector. The paper further shows why: 69% of annotated unfaithfulness occurs on incorrect answers, so much of apparent detection performance is really accuracy prediction.

Second, after stratification, the task splits cleanly into two regimes. On the complete-feature subset, the correct-answer regime has n=363 examples. There, behavioral signals provide moderate separation between faithful reasoning and post-hoc rationalization, with AUROC around 0.63–0.67. The benchmark’s own step-removal metric even points in the wrong direction. The incorrect-answer regime has n=270 examples and contains most of the unfaithfulness; in this regime, none of the tested behavioral signals performs detectably above chance.

Third, internal and behavioral evidence diverge. Linear probes over hidden states can decode the incorrect-answer regime in Llama-3.1-8B, with nested held-out AUROC 0.67 and selection-corrected permutation p≤0.03. In Qwen-2.5-7B, probes decode the correct-answer regime with p=0.014. Crucially, probes trained on one regime do not transfer to the other. The paper therefore finds no shared positively aligned linear direction across regimes, suggesting that “unfaithfulness” is not a single phenomenon.

Fourth, on synthetic data, the authors compare instructed answer-first prompting across seven open models with hint-induced rationalization in the style of Turpin et al. Both are internally detectable on their own constructed traces, but only the hint-induced variant transfers to the human-annotated benchmark, and it transfers to the incorrect-answer regime rather than the superficially similar correct-answer post-hoc regime. Instructed rationalization transfers to neither regime. That is a direct caution against the common practice of training or evaluating detectors on synthetic unfaithful CoT distributions without checking transfer.42:T42f,A good skim order is by argument structure, not by detector family. Start with the introduction and lock onto the main claim: behavioral detection is mostly reading answer correctness. Then read the section that resolves the faithful_type label discrepancy; without that, the regime split is hard to trust. After that, go straight to the correctness-stratified results, which are the most informative part of the paper.
